I’ve had low stretches on and off, but I haven’t noticed it being most of the day on most days for the whole year. It’s been more vague than that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you had days when your mood felt low or depressed for most of the day, and if so, how often did that happen?\n\nPatient: I’ve had days like that, yes, where I felt low for a good part of the day. But I’m not sure how often. It wasn’t something I kept track of, and I don’t think I could honestly give a number or pattern.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, would you say there have been more days where your mood felt low or depressed for most of the day than days where it didn’t?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say there were more low days than not. There have been rough periods, but I haven’t tracked it closely enough to say that.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a time when you felt down or depressed for several days in a row? How did it feel, and how long did it last?\n\nPatient: I can’t really give a clear example. There have been a few stretches where I felt kind of heavy or down for several days, maybe a little withdrawn, but I don’t remember exactly how long they lasted. It wasn’t something I was tracking, so I’m hesitant to say it was a specific number of days.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there were any specific weeks or periods in the last year when you felt down or low nearly every day, even if it wasn’t severe?\n\nPatient: I can’t really identify a specific week like that. I know there were periods where I felt low more often than usual, but I don’t remember it clearly enough to say it was nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Since this started about a year ago, have you generally felt down or low most of the day, on most days, even if it hasn't always been severe?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say that, no. I’ve had low stretches on and off, but I haven’t noticed it being most of the day on most days for the whole year. It’s been more vague than that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you had days when your mood felt low or depressed for most of the day, and if so, how often did that happen?\n\nPatient: I’ve had days like that, yes, where I felt low for a good part of the day. But I’m not sure how often. It wasn’t something I kept track of, and I don’t think I could honestly give a number or pattern.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, would you say there have been more days where your mood felt low or depressed for most of the day than days where it didn’t?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say there were more low days than not. It’s felt uneven, with some low days and some okay days, but I haven’t really tracked it closely enough to compare them.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you had periods where your mood felt low or depressed for most of the day, on most days, even if it wasn't severe?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I’ve had stretches where I felt low a lot of the time, but I’m not sure I can say it was most of the day, most days. I didn’t really keep track of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you had times when your mood felt low or depressed for most of the day, even if it wasn’t severe, and if so, how often did that happen?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how often. There have been times when I felt down for a lot of the day, but I didn’t keep track, and I can’t honestly say whether it was happening regularly or just in patches.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’ve been feeling like I don’t deserve the trust people put in me. I keep going over small things from work—little clinical imperfections—and treating them like proof that I’m dangerous as a doctor. It’s been hard to get past that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or like you’re a bad or unworthy person, even when there’s no real reason for it?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do feel guilty, more than I think makes sense. I keep taking small things from work—little imperfections in clinical decisions or documentation—and replaying them like they mean I’m unsafe or shouldn’t be trusted as a doctor. It feels like proof that I’m unworthy, even when part of me knows it may not be that clear.\n\nDoctor: Have you had experiences like this before, where you felt guilty or unworthy, or is this the first time you’ve felt this way?\n\nPatient: It’s not completely new, but it feels stronger now. I’ve had moments before where I was hard on myself, but lately I keep coming back to this feeling that I don’t deserve people’s trust. I replay small clinical things over and over, like they prove I’m dangerous as a doctor, even when I know they were probably minor.\n\nDoctor: When you say this has happened before but feels stronger now, were there clear times in the past when you felt completely free of these feelings, or has it been more of a constant struggle with some periods being worse than others?\n\nPatient: It’s been more like it started and then stayed with me. I think it began after I received the clinic award—about a year and a month ago. Since then it hasn’t really fully gone away, though some periods feel worse than others.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been low or depressed most of the time, nearly every day, since this started?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say it’s been low most of the time every day. I’ve had a lot of guilt and worry about myself, but whether my mood has been consistently depressed like that… I don’t know. I haven’t really tracked it that way.\n\nDoctor: Since this started about a year ago, have you had a low or depressed mood most days, even if it wasn’t severe every day?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I know I’ve felt weighed down by it a lot, but I don’t think I’ve been keeping track well enough to say it was most days. Some days I can function and feel more normal, and other days it’s more present.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I can’t clearly point to a two-week stretch where I was depressed most of the day every day. There have been rough periods, but I haven’t tracked it closely enough to say that.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a time when you felt down or depressed for several days in a row? How did it feel, and how long did it last?\n\nPatient: I can’t really give a clear example. There have been a few stretches where I felt kind of heavy or down for several days, maybe a little withdrawn, but I don’t remember exactly how long they lasted. It wasn’t something I was tracking, so I’m hesitant to say it was a specific number of days.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there were any specific weeks or periods in the last year when you felt down or low nearly every day, even if it wasn’t severe?\n\nPatient: I can’t really identify a specific week like that. I know there were periods where I felt low more often than usual, but I don’t remember it clearly enough to say it was nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Since this started about a year ago, have you generally felt down or low most of the day, on most days, even if it hasn't always been severe?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say that, no. I’ve had low stretches on and off, but I haven’t noticed it being most of the day on most days for the whole year. It’s been more vague than that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you had days when your mood felt low or depressed for most of the day, and if so, how often did that happen?\n\nPatient: I’ve had days like that, yes, where I felt low for a good part of the day. But I’m not sure how often. It wasn’t something I kept track of, and I don’t think I could honestly give a number or pattern.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, would you say there have been more days where your mood felt low or depressed for most of the day than days where it didn’t?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say there were more low days than not. It’s felt uneven, with some low days and some okay days, but I haven’t really tracked it closely enough to compare them.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you had periods where your mood felt low or depressed for most of the day, on most days, even if it wasn't severe?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I’ve had stretches where I felt low a lot of the time, but I’m not sure I can say it was most of the day, most days. There have been times when I felt down for a lot of the day, but I didn’t keep track, and I can’t honestly say whether it was happening regularly or just in patches.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had a low mood for most of the day, more days than not, even if it wasn’t severely depressed?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say that for sure. Looking back over two years, there were definitely periods where I felt low, but I haven’t noticed it as being most of the day, more days than not.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had a low mood that lasted most of the day, more days than not?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so—not in a way I can clearly say was most of the day, more days than not. I’ve had low periods, but I haven’t tracked it closely enough to be sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I keep taking small things from work—little imperfections in clinical decisions or documentation—and replaying them like they mean I’m unsafe or shouldn’t be trusted as a doctor. It feels like proof that I’m unworthy, even when part of me knows it may not be that clear.\n\nDoctor: Have you had experiences like this before, where you felt guilty or unworthy, or is this the first time you’ve felt this way?\n\nPatient: It’s not completely new, but it feels stronger now. I’ve had moments before where I was hard on myself, but lately I keep coming back to this feeling that I don’t deserve people’s trust. I replay small clinical things over and over, like they prove I’m dangerous as a doctor, even when I know they were probably minor.\n\nDoctor: When you say this has happened before but feels stronger now, were there clear times in the past when you felt completely free of these feelings, or has it been more of a constant struggle with some periods being worse than others?\n\nPatient: It’s been more like it started and then stayed with me. I think it began after I received the clinic award—about a year and a month ago. Since then it hasn’t really fully gone away, though some periods feel worse than others.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been low or depressed most of the time, nearly every day, since this started?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say it’s been low most of the time every day. I’ve had a lot of guilt and worry about myself, but whether my mood has been consistently depressed like that… I don’t know. I haven’t really tracked it that way.\n\nDoctor: Since this started about a year ago, have you had a low or depressed mood most days, even if it wasn’t severe every day?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I know I’ve felt weighed down by it a lot, but I don’t think I’ve been keeping track well enough to say it was most days. Some days I can function and feel more normal, and other days it’s more present.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I can’t clearly point to a two-week stretch where I was depressed most of the day every day. There have been rough periods, but I haven’t tracked it closely enough to say that.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a time when you felt down or depressed for several days in a row? How did it feel, and how long did it last?\n\nPatient: I can’t really give a clear example. There have been a few stretches where I felt kind of heavy or down for several days, maybe a little withdrawn, but I don’t remember exactly how long they lasted. It wasn’t something I was tracking, so I’m hesitant to say it was a specific number of days.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there were any specific weeks or periods in the last year when you felt down or low nearly every day, even if it wasn’t severe?\n\nPatient: I can’t really identify a specific week like that. I know there were periods where I felt low more often than usual, but I don’t remember it clearly enough to say it was nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Since this started about a year ago, have you generally felt down or low most of the day, on most days, even if it hasn't always been severe?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say that, no. I’ve had low stretches on and off, but I haven’t noticed it being most of the day on most days for the whole year. It’s been more vague than that.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you had days when your mood felt low or depressed for most of the day, and if so, how often did that happen?\n\nPatient: I’ve had days like that, yes, where I felt low for a good part of the day. But I’m not sure how often. It wasn’t something I kept track of, and I don’t think I could honestly give a number or pattern.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, would you say there have been more days where your mood felt low or depressed for most of the day than days where it didn’t?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say there were more low days than not. Looking back over two years, there were definitely periods where I felt low, but I haven’t noticed it as being most of the day, more days than not.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had a low mood that lasted most of the day, more days than not?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so—not in a way I can clearly say was most of the day, more days than not.
